promised."}],"channel_subchannels_anchored_here":[{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Measured Ascent and Collapsing Distance","parent_semantic_invariant":"A remote-looking event is recalibrated by a higher scale whose route, duration, and endpoint make it near.","parent_surprising_reach":"The scale is not only numerical; it becomes a route with bends, halts, visible heights, and an endpoint that closes distance.","key":"B","name":"Bent route to a visible height","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A route inclines, bends, pauses, and rises until it reaches an exposed high place.","active_motifs":"bent course `quranic:root_000997:B002/m01`; halt on a route `quranic:root_000997:B003/m01`; upward passage `quranic:root_000997:B004/m01`; elevation `quranic:root_000745:B001/m01`; distant visible height `quranic:root_000745:B002/m01`; overhead canopy `quranic:root_000745:B004/m01`; raised rank `quranic:root_000751:B003/m01`; flood-safe high ground `quranic:root_001476:B004/m01`; elevated cloud or ground `quranic:root_001675:B016/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:3-4` مَعَارِج/تَعْرُج (`ع ر ج`); `70:4` سَنَة (`س ن و`); `70:8` السَّمَاء (`س م و`); `70:14` يُنْجِيه (`ن ج و`).","synthesis":"The lexical path is not a featureless vertical line. It has inclination, stopping points, and visible high ground, so ascent becomes a traversed geography. The high refuge motif also pressures the rescue wish in `70:14`: even terrain above a flood belongs to the same measured route.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:3","70:4","70:8","70:14"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] The Falling Event as Hydraulic Breach","parent_semantic_invariant":"What falls moves like a fluid surge through channels, pools, and barriers until defenses cease to regulate it.","parent_surprising_reach":"The punishment acquires the mechanics of rain, flood discharge, reservoirs, and failed waterworks.","key":"A","name":"Rainfall, discharge, and catchment","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Water falls, arrives in a single discharge, crowds into a surge, runs through outlets, and settles in catchments.","active_motifs":"falling rain `quranic:root_001675:B003/m01`; water-holding hollow `quranic:root_001675:B008/m01`; one burst of liquid `quranic:root_000480:B004/m01`; flood and wave surge `quranic:root_000480:B005/m01`; water channels and outfalls `quranic:root_000480:B006/m01`; flood barrier with measured release `quranic:root_000751:B007/m01`; mountain channel `quranic:root_001159:B006/m01`; waterskin `quranic:root_001212:B009/m01`; hot water and spring `quranic:root_000001:B002/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:2` دَافِع (`د ف ع`); `70:4` سَنَة (`س ن و`); `70:7` قَرِيب (`ق ر ب`); `70:10` حَمِيم (`ح م م`); `70:13` فَصِيلَتِه (`ف ص ل`).","synthesis":"Falling becomes a complete hydraulic sequence: precipitation, pulse, surge, channel, and basin. The hot-water motif converts an ordinary catchment into a punitive reservoir, while the mountain channel anticipates terrain that can no longer hold its form.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:2","70:4","70:7","70:10","70:13"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] The Falling Event as Hydraulic Breach","parent_semantic_invariant":"What falls moves like a fluid surge through channels, pools, and barriers until defenses cease to regulate it.","parent_surprising_reach":"The punishment acquires the mechanics of rain, flood discharge, reservoirs, and failed waterworks.","key":"B","name":"Barrier and repeller failure","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"Protective works try to divert, delay, wall off, or stopper an arriving force, but the event is fixed and severe.","active_motifs":"diverting a harmful force `quranic:root_000480:B001/m01`; delaying a claim `quranic:root_000480:B008/m01`; controlled flood barrier `quranic:root_000751:B007/m01`; stopper for a vessel or well `quranic:root_000840:B018/m01`; covered mountain pass or low wall `quranic:root_001307:B013/m01`; short defensive wall `quranic:root_001159:B007/m01`; fixed falling occurrence `quranic:root_001675:B001/m01`; severe descending event `quranic:root_001675:B002/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:2` دَافِع (`د ف ع`); `70:4` سَنَة (`س ن و`); `70:5` اصْبِر (`ص ب ر`); `70:2` كَافِرِين (`ك ف ر`); `70:13` فَصِيلَتِه (`ف ص ل`).","synthesis":"Diversion, postponement, gate, stopper, pass, and wall are successive control mechanisms. Their assembly sharpens “no repeller”: the failure is not lack of one heroic defender but the exhaustion of every ordinary means by which a flow is checked.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:2","70:4","70:5","70:13"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] The Falling Event as Hydraulic Breach","parent_semantic_invariant":"What falls moves like a fluid surge through channels, pools, and barriers until defenses cease to regulate it.","parent_surprising_reach":"The punishment acquires the mechanics of rain, flood discharge, reservoirs, and failed waterworks.","key":"C","name":"Watering interval, thirst, and pre-birth flow","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A herd approaches water on a counted cycle while thirst, irrigation, milk, and visible pregnancy regulate access to life-giving fluid.","active_motifs":"five-day camel watering cycle `quranic:root_000439:B003/m01`; alternating camel-water schedule `quranic:root_000997:B006/m01`; sweet potable water `quranic:root_000994:B001/m01`; abstention from food and drink `quranic:root_000994:B002/m01`; irrigation by drawing water `quranic:root_000751:B001/m01`; night approach to a water source `quranic:root_001212:B008/m01`; milk entering the udder before birth `quranic:root_000480:B009/m01`; visible animal pregnancy `quranic:root_000531:B010/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1,11` عَذَاب (`ع ذ ب`); `70:2` دَافِع (`د ف ع`); `70:3-4` مَعَارِج/تَعْرُج (`ع ر ج`); `70:4` خَمْسِين، سَنَة (`خ م س`, `س ن و`); `70:6-7` يَرَوْن/نَرَى، قَرِيب (`ر ء ي`, `ق ر ب`).","synthesis":"Counted time becomes a husbandry schedule rather than an abstraction. A thirsty herd, drawn irrigation, approach to the source, and milk arriving before birth form a life-maintaining cycle that the fifty-thousand-year measure radically enlarges.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:2","70:3","70:4","70:6"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Thermal Unmaking of Sky, Mountain, and Body","parent_semantic_invariant":"Heat converts stable forms into melt, fiber, cooked flesh, and stripped residue.","parent_surprising_reach":"Cosmic collapse is rendered through kitchen, foundry, textile, and anatomical operations.","key":"C","name":"Roasting, stripping, and terminal residue","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"Heat cooks the body, extraction pulls it from its seat, and only a diminished remainder survives.","active_motifs":"roasting by heat `quranic:root_000830:B001/m01`; extremities and scalp `quranic:root_000830:B002/m01`; remaining scrap `quranic:root_000830:B004/m01`; pulling from a fixed place `quranic:root_001489:B001/m01`; receded hairline `quranic:root_001489:B007/m01`; death throes `quranic:root_001489:B016/m01`; punitive pain `quranic:root_000994:B005/m01`; dangling lash or strip `quranic:root_000994:B006/m01`; audible impact `quranic:root_001675:B004/m01`; sharpened blade `quranic:root_001675:B009/m01`; cutting and shearing `quranic:root_000239:B001/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1,11` عَذَاب (`ع ذ ب`); `70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:11` الْمُجْرِم (`ج ر م`); `70:16` نَزَّاعَة، الشَّوَى (`ن ز ع`, `ش و ي`).","synthesis":"Roasting, blade impact, shearing, pulling, and scalp recession give the fire a precise dismantling sequence. The “extremities” are neither generic flesh nor an incidental detail: they are the nonfatal edges repeatedly removed until residue and death-throes converge.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:11","70:16"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Restraint, Enclosure, and the Loss of an Exit","parent_semantic_invariant":"Holding can be ethical self-command, protective enclosure, coercive confinement, or pathological pressure.","parent_surprising_reach":"The same holding operation moves from beautiful patience to stopper, wall, armor, shackle, sealed wound, and crookedly healed bone.","key":"A","name":"Beautiful inward restraint","reading_type":"surface-primary","scene_or_process":"The self remains held together by deliberate non-panic and withdrawal from an attached impulse.","active_motifs":"holding the self against panic `quranic:root_000840:B001/m01`; beautiful conduct `quranic:root_000260:B004/m01`; abstaining or weaning from a thing `quranic:root_000994:B003/m01`; ceasing after attachment `quranic:root_001489:B003/m01`; courage that does not leave its opponent `quranic:root_001390:B004/m01`; no alternative or turning away `quranic:root_001664:B006/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:2` لَيْس (`ل ي س`); `70:5` اصْبِر صَبْرًا جَمِيلًا (`ص ب ر`, `ج م ل`); `70:1,11` عَذَاب (`ع ذ ب`); `70:16` نَزَّاعَة (`ن ز ع`); `70:18` أَوْعَى (`و ع ي`).","synthesis":"Patience is an active containment system: the self is held, appetite is weaned, attachment is released, and position is not abandoned. “Beautiful” names the quality of this controlled holding, not an ornamental mood.","ayah_refs":["70:2","70:5","70:1","70:16","70:18"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Restraint, Enclosure, and the Loss of an Exit","parent_semantic_invariant":"Holding can be ethical self-command, protective enclosure, coercive confinement, or pathological pressure.","parent_surprising_reach":"The same holding operation moves from beautiful patience to stopper, wall, armor, shackle, sealed wound, and crookedly healed bone.","key":"C","name":"Pressure sealed inside the body","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A break, sore, or wound closes badly while heat and pus remain trapped within it.","active_motifs":"bone knitting after fracture, even crookedly `quranic:root_001664:B003/m01`; pus filling a wound `quranic:root_001664:B004/m01`; suppurating ulcer `quranic:root_000025:B011/m01`; saddle sore on the back `quranic:root_000458:B017/m01`; riding lesion `quranic:root_001675:B010/m01`; bodily fever `quranic:root_000001:B005/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:10` حَمِيم (`ح م م`); `70:14` الْأَرْض (`ء ر ض`); `70:17` أَدْبَر (`د ب ر`); `70:18` أَوْعَى (`و ع ي`).","synthesis":"Containment becomes pathological when closure preserves what should be discharged. A crookedly joined bone and a pus-filled sore mirror the hoard: both are accumulations sealed into a body until holding itself becomes injury.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:10","70:14","70:17","70:18"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Question, Summons, and Failed Response","parent_semantic_invariant":"Speech seeks, calls, or demands a response, but the only effective answer is the event itself.","parent_surprising_reach":"Petition, riddle, curse, legal claim, and cascading collapse become alternate forms of a summons that cannot be negotiated.","key":"A","name":"Request, desired object, and mutual inquiry","reading_type":"surface-primary","scene_or_process":"An asker formulates a demand, names its desired object, seeks fulfillment, and may exchange questions with another.","active_motifs":"asking and requesting `quranic:root_000661:B001/m01`; desired object or need `quranic:root_000661:B002/m01`; satisfying a request `quranic:root_000661:B003/m01`; reciprocal questioning `quranic:root_000661:B004/m01`; interrogative or relative “what” `quranic:root_000527:B004/m01`; attention-seeking “tell me” `quranic:root_000531:B013/m01`; enigmatic question `quranic:root_000478:B007/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1,10` سَأَل/سَائِل/يَسْأَل (`س ء ل`); `70:6-7` يَرَوْن/نَرَى (`ر ء ي`); `70:17` تَدْعُو (`د ع و`); surface anchor unavailable for `ذ و و` in `70:3`.","synthesis":"Asking unfolds into requester, sought object, possible fulfillment, and reciprocal dialogue. The interrogative and riddle motifs expose the structural reversal: the opening question is answered, but not through the social exchange that question normally initiates.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:6","70:17","70:3"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Question, Summons, and Failed Response","parent_semantic_invariant":"Speech seeks, calls, or demands a response, but the only effective answer is the event itself.","parent_surprising_reach":"Petition, riddle, curse, legal claim, and cascading collapse become alternate forms of a summons that cannot be negotiated.","key":"B","name":"The petitioner who is pushed away","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A needy claimant is repelled, delayed, and excluded rather than having the request discharged.","active_motifs":"socially rejected petitioner `quranic:root_000480:B007/m01`; procrastinated claim `quranic:root_000480:B008/m01`; request for need `quranic:root_000661:B001/m01`; need fulfilled `quranic:root_000661:B003/m01`; emphatic negation `quranic:root_001390:B001/m01`; excluding exception `quranic:root_001390:B002/m01`; remote isolated settlement `quranic:root_001307:B012/m01`; mercy toward one seeking shelter `quranic:root_000070:B002/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1,10` سَائِل/يَسْأَل (`س ء ل`); `70:2` لَيْس، دَافِع، كَافِرِين (`ل ي س`, `د ف ع`, `ك ف ر`); `70:13` تُؤْوِيه (`ء و ي`).","synthesis":"The no-repeller formula acquires a social underside: a petitioner is physically pushed aside, a claim is stalled, and negation closes the case. The opening asker and the later failure of intimates to ask are held together by the collapse of ordinary petition.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:2","70:13"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Question, Summons, and Failed Response","parent_semantic_invariant":"Speech seeks, calls, or demands a response, but the only effective answer is the event itself.","parent_surprising_reach":"Petition, riddle, curse, legal claim, and cascading collapse become alternate forms of a summons that cannot be negotiated.","key":"C","name":"Calling down the event","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A voice calls toward a target, invokes harm, and is followed by one collapse after another.","active_motifs":"vocal summons `quranic:root_000478:B001/m01`; calling down harm `quranic:root_000478:B004/m01`; successive falling or demolition `quranic:root_000478:B005/m01`; vicissitudes that pull a person `quranic:root_000478:B006/m01`; severe descending event `quranic:root_001675:B002/m01`; extermination without remnant `quranic:root_000458:B004/m01`; surviving remainder `quranic:root_000830:B004/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:16` الشَّوَى (`ش و ي`); `70:17` تَدْعُو، أَدْبَر (`د ع و`, `د ب ر`).","synthesis":"The fire's call is both voice and causation. Invocation is followed by sequential collapse, while the polarity between total extermination and residual scrap defines what remains after the summons reaches those who turned away.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:16","70:17"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Visibility, Bodily Evidence, and Inscribed Judgment","parent_semantic_invariant":"What was estimated, hidden, or relationally ignored is made perceptible through sight, marks, seams, blood, and text.","parent_surprising_reach":"Visual disclosure extends into mirror, banner, blood trace, stitched edge, textual division, and signature.","key":"A","name":"Forced mutual visibility","reading_type":"surface-primary","scene_or_process":"Observers face one another and are made to see what their prior estimate displaced.","active_motifs":"sensory and inward sight `quranic:root_000531:B001/m01`; considered perception `quranic:root_000531:B002/m01`; mutual facing within sight `quranic:root_000531:B004/m01`; mirror and visible aspect `quranic:root_000531:B006/m01`; making another see `quranic:root_000531:B012/m01`; ocular vision `quranic:root_000121:B001/m01`; inward discernment `quranic:root_000121:B002/m01`; waiting for what is expected `quranic:root_001675:B012/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:6-7` يَرَوْنَه/نَرَاه (`ر ء ي`); `70:11` يُبَصَّرُونَهُم (`ب ص ر`).","synthesis":"Seeing progresses from perception, to estimate, to mutual facing, to compelled display. The mirror motif removes the safety of a one-sided gaze: the criminal sees the relatives and, in the same scene, becomes visible to them.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:6","70:11"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Visibility, Bodily Evidence, and Inscribed Judgment","parent_semantic_invariant":"What was estimated, hidden, or relationally ignored is made perceptible through sight, marks, seams, blood, and text.","parent_surprising_reach":"Visual disclosure extends into mirror, banner, blood trace, stitched edge, textual division, and signature.","key":"B","name":"Mark, division, and written closure","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A matter is made legible by banner, articulated speech, textual segmentation, detail, and a final written mark.","active_motifs":"visible standard `quranic:root_000531:B011/m01`; tongue that distinguishes matters `quranic:root_001159:B004/m01`; textual and metrical divisions `quranic:root_001159:B011/m01`; analytic detailing `quranic:root_001159:B013/m01`; grammatical separator `quranic:root_001159:B015/m01`; signature or marginal notation `quranic:root_001675:B011/m01`; projected voice `quranic:root_000239:B008/m01`; alphabetic letter `quranic:root_000045:B006/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:4` أَلْف (`ء ل ف`); `70:6-7` يَرَوْن/نَرَى (`ر ء ي`); `70:11` الْمُجْرِم (`ج ر م`); `70:13` فَصِيلَتِه (`ف ص ل`).","synthesis":"Judgment becomes an act of legibility. A standard points, a tongue distinguishes, divisions organize, detail discloses components, and a signature closes the record. The relatives listed in discrete sequence read like the articulated sections of the criminal's liability.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:4","70:6","70:11","70:13"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Visibility, Bodily Evidence, and Inscribed Judgment","parent_semantic_invariant":"What was estimated, hidden, or relationally ignored is made perceptible through sight, marks, seams, blood, and text.","parent_surprising_reach":"Visual disclosure extends into mirror, banner, blood trace, stitched edge, textual division, and signature.","key":"C","name":"Blood trace as testimony","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Bodily fluid lands, leaves a visible trace, and records the force that produced it.","active_motifs":"blood trace on body or ground `quranic:root_000121:B004/m01`; menstrual sign distinguishing states `quranic:root_000531:B007/m01`; one discharge of blood or liquid `quranic:root_000480:B004/m01`; impact mark and sound `quranic:root_001675:B004/m01`; cutting offense `quranic:root_000239:B001/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:2` دَافِع (`د ف ع`); `70:6-7` يَرَوْن/نَرَى (`ر ء ي`); `70:11` يُبَصَّرُون، الْمُجْرِم (`ب ص ر`, `ج ر م`).","synthesis":"Vision is not confined to an eye. A fluid sign and a blood-mark preserve an event on the body or ground, while the impact supplies its cause. The scene turns punishment into evidence that can be inspected rather than denied.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:2","70:6","70:11"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Caravan Order and the Wounded Mount","parent_semantic_invariant":"A herd survives by ordered leadership, water, route, and tack, yet the same journey leaves the bearing animal abraded and spent.","parent_surprising_reach":"The procession includes a herd leader, valley centerline, water reserve, honored mount, saddle underlay, and paired riding wounds.","key":"A","name":"Herd, leader, and return","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A collected herd follows a leading animal and returns toward its managed resting place.","active_motifs":"camel herd with keepers `quranic:root_000260:B001/m01`; large camel group `quranic:root_000997:B005/m01`; leading animal followed by the rest `quranic:root_001444:B008/m01`; livestock returning in the afternoon `quranic:root_000609:B005/m01`; animals kneeling to rest `quranic:root_001675:B007/m01`; capable management of livestock wealth `quranic:root_001056:B007/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:3-4` مَعَارِج/تَعْرُج (`ع ر ج`); `70:4` الرُّوح، مَلَائِكَة (`ر و ح`, `م ل ك`); `70:5` جَمِيلًا (`ج م ل`); `70:9` الْعِهْن (`ع ه ن`).","synthesis":"The ascent is reframed as a managed procession: herd, leader, keepers, timed return, and kneeling place. Order depends on following the guide; turning away from the ordered line in `70:17` is therefore a pastoral as well as moral dislocation.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:3","70:4","70:5","70:9"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Caravan Order and the Wounded Mount","parent_semantic_invariant":"A herd survives by ordered leadership, water, route, and tack, yet the same journey leaves the bearing animal abraded and spent.","parent_surprising_reach":"The procession includes a herd leader, valley centerline, water reserve, honored mount, saddle underlay, and paired riding wounds.","key":"B","name":"Route, tack, and abrasion","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A mount follows the center of a route with water enough for travel, protected by tack that nonetheless produces sores.","active_motifs":"ship-rope or heavy lead cord `quranic:root_000260:B002/m01`; saddle underlay `quranic:root_001684:B011/m01`; saddle sore `quranic:root_000458:B017/m01`; riding abrasion `quranic:root_001675:B010/m01`; centerline of road or valley `quranic:root_001444:B006/m01`; water that sustains a traveler `quranic:root_001444:B007/m01`; prepared honored mount `quranic:root_001212:B013/m01`; camel exempted from burden `quranic:root_000480:B010/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:2` دَافِع (`د ف ع`); `70:4` مَلَائِكَة (`م ل ك`); `70:5` جَمِيلًا (`ج م ل`); `70:7` قَرِيب (`ق ر ب`); `70:17` أَدْبَر، تَوَلَّى (`د ب ر`, `و ل ي`).","synthesis":"Route and tack distribute a journey's load: centerline guides, water sustains, underlay cushions, and an honored mount is spared work. The paired riding sores expose the failure of that distribution under extreme duration: what carries the procession is itself marked by the passage.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:2","70:4","70:5","70:7","70:17"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Gestation, Weaning, and the Severed Offspring Bond","parent_semantic_invariant":"Life is joined through mating and gestation, then differentiated through birth, weaning, and maturation.","parent_surprising_reach":"The family list expands into animal breeding, visible pregnancy, uterine tissue, milk-before-birth, weaning, and the adult child becoming a companion.","key":"A","name":"Mating, pregnancy, and pre-birth provision","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"A male mounts, pregnancy becomes visible, birth approaches, and milk enters the udder in advance.","active_motifs":"stallion mounting a herd `quranic:root_000745:B003/m01`; male camel covering the female `quranic:root_000751:B010/m01`; horse becoming breeding male `quranic:root_000609:B018/m01`; visible animal pregnancy `quranic:root_000531:B010/m01`; imminent birth `quranic:root_001212:B012/m01`; milk before parturition `quranic:root_000480:B009/m01`; uterine vessels `quranic:root_001056:B005/m01`; womb and post-birth discharge `quranic:root_000994:B009/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1,11` عَذَاب (`ع ذ ب`); `70:2` دَافِع (`د ف ع`); `70:4` الرُّوح، سَنَة (`ر و ح`, `س ن و`); `70:6-7` يَرَوْن/نَرَى، قَرِيب (`ر ء ي`, `ق ر ب`); `70:8` السَّمَاء (`س م و`); `70:9` الْعِهْن (`ع ه ن`).","synthesis":"Reproduction is a causal chain rather than a family label: mating, conception, visible swelling, approaching birth, uterine structure, and anticipatory milk. The son's presence in `70:11` carries this whole material history, intensifying the violence of offering him as an exchange object.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:2","70:4","70:6","70:8","70:9"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Cultivation, Cutting, and the Hoarded Harvest","parent_semantic_invariant":"Rain and soil produce covered growth that is cut, sorted, and gathered into post-harvest stock.","parent_surprising_reach":"The hoard resembles a crop economy whose seed-covering, irrigation, palm propagation, cutting, gleaning, drying, and piling have been detached from redistribution.","key":"A","name":"Soil, rain, and emerging growth","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Rain reaches fertile ground, plants root and unfold, while dry growth marks the opposite state.","active_motifs":"fertile planted earth `quranic:root_000025:B002/m01`; rainfall `quranic:root_001675:B003/m01`; leaf and aromatic plant `quranic:root_000609:B012/m01`; tree bursting into leaf `quranic:root_000609:B017/m01`; named herb `quranic:root_001489:B015/m01`; dry tree `quranic:root_000217:B008/m01`; green tanning shrub `quranic:root_000076:B005/m01`; medicinal senna plant `quranic:root_000751:B005/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:4` الرُّوح، سَنَة (`ر و ح`, `س ن و`); `70:9` الْجِبَال (`ج ب ل`); `70:14` الْأَرْض (`ء ر ض`); `70:16` نَزَّاعَة (`ن ز ع`); surface anchor unavailable for `ء ل ي` at `70:4`.","synthesis":"The earth is not inert inventory. It receives rain, holds rooted growth, opens leaves, and yields named plants; drought produces the dry-tree counterstate. Offering “all on earth” would therefore consume a productive ecology, not merely a territorial expanse.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:4","70:9","70:14","70:16"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Cultivation, Cutting, and the Hoarded Harvest","parent_semantic_invariant":"Rain and soil produce covered growth that is cut, sorted, and gathered into post-harvest stock.","parent_surprising_reach":"The hoard resembles a crop economy whose seed-covering, irrigation, palm propagation, cutting, gleaning, drying, and piling have been detached from redistribution.","key":"B","name":"Covering seed and governing water","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Seed is covered, fruit remains sheathed, and irrigation is admitted through regulated barriers into protected catchments.","active_motifs":"sowing by covering seed `quranic:root_001307:B008/m01`; fruit sheath `quranic:root_001307:B010/m01`; drawing irrigation water `quranic:root_000751:B001/m01`; flood-control barrier `quranic:root_000751:B007/m01`; water-holding ground `quranic:root_001675:B008/m01`; raised ground above flood `quranic:root_001476:B004/m01`; mountain watercourse `quranic:root_001159:B006/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:2` كَافِرِين (`ك ف ر`); `70:4` سَنَة (`س ن و`); `70:13` فَصِيلَتِه (`ف ص ل`); `70:14` يُنْجِيه (`ن ج و`).","synthesis":"Productive enclosure differs from hoarding: soil covers seed to germinate, a sheath protects fruit, and a barrier releases only needed water. These regulated containers expose the moral defect of accumulation in `70:18`, which closes without a life-giving outlet.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:2","70:4","70:13","70:14"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Animated Body, Suppurating Wound, and Death-Pull","parent_semantic_invariant":"The living body is an assembled form sustained by breath, then marked by heat and injury before animation is withdrawn.","parent_surprising_reach":"Breath, lung, bodily frame, fever, sweat, saddle sore, pus, and death-throe form a complete deterioration sequence.","key":"B","name":"Heat, sweat, sore, and pus","reading_type":"latent/lexical","scene_or_process":"Heat produces sweat and fever; repeated bearing opens sores that retain corrupt fluid.","active_motifs":"sweat under heat `quranic:root_000001:B004/m01`; bodily fever `quranic:root_000001:B005/m01`; saddle lesion `quranic:root_000458:B017/m01`; riding abrasion `quranic:root_001675:B010/m01`; suppurating ulcer `quranic:root_000025:B011/m01`; pus-filled wound `quranic:root_001664:B004/m01`; blood trace `quranic:root_000121:B004/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:10` حَمِيم (`ح م م`); `70:11` يُبَصَّرُون (`ب ص ر`); `70:14` الْأَرْض (`ء ر ض`); `70:17` أَدْبَر (`د ب ر`); `70:18` أَوْعَى (`و ع ي`).","synthesis":"The bodily scene converts judgment's heat into a medical process: sweat, fever, abrasion, ulcer, pus, and visible blood. Accumulation returns here as trapped corrupt matter, joining the wounded body to the sealed hoard.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:10","70:11","70:14","70:17","70:18"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Sovereign Capacity, Crime, and Unaccepted Surety","parent_semantic_invariant":"A fixed penalty issues from effective dominion, distinguishes liability, and cannot be neutralized by private ownership or guarantee.","parent_surprising_reach":"Divine title, royal rule, calibrated design, adjudication, criminal gain, surety, and retribution form one legal order.","key":"B","name":"Adjudication and criminal liability","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"An offense is identified, judgment separates truth from false claim, and retribution reaches completion.","active_motifs":"decisive judgment `quranic:root_001159:B002/m01`; offense and criminality `quranic:root_000239:B004/m01`; gain that incurs responsibility `quranic:root_000239:B003/m01`; fixed obligation `quranic:root_001675:B001/m01`; severe judicial event `quranic:root_001675:B002/m01`; retributive equivalence `quranic:root_000840:B012/m01`; decreed outcome `quranic:root_000001:B010/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:5` صَبْر (`ص ب ر`); `70:10` حَمِيم (`ح م م`); `70:11` الْمُجْرِم (`ج ر م`); `70:13` فَصِيلَتِه (`ف ص ل`).","synthesis":"Crime is linked to acquisition, then passed through decisive separation and equivalent requital. The falling event is thus a judgment that has moved from liability to execution, not an unclassified catastrophe.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:5","70:10","70:11","70:13"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Sovereign Capacity, Crime, and Unaccepted Surety","parent_semantic_invariant":"A fixed penalty issues from effective dominion, distinguishes liability, and cannot be neutralized by private ownership or guarantee.","parent_surprising_reach":"Divine title, royal rule, calibrated design, adjudication, criminal gain, surety, and retribution form one legal order.","key":"C","name":"Surety that cannot answer","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"Owner, guarantor, and protector are all proposed, but none has standing to discharge the claim.","active_motifs":"personal surety `quranic:root_000840:B003/m01`; undertaking another's charge `quranic:root_001332:B003/m01`; ownership and transferable control `quranic:root_001444:B002/m01`; diversion of harm `quranic:root_000480:B001/m01`; emphatic absence `quranic:root_001390:B001/m01`; fulfilling a petition `quranic:root_000661:B003/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1` سَائِل (`س ء ل`); `70:2` لَيْس، دَافِع (`ل ي س`, `د ف ع`); `70:4` كَان، مَلَائِكَة (`ك و ن`, `م ل ك`); `70:5` صَبْر (`ص ب ر`).","synthesis":"The legal scene tests each possible answer to liability: surety, custodian, owner, defender, and satisfied claimant. Emphatic negation rejects the entire chain, which is why even universal ransom in `70:14` remains ineffective.","ayah_refs":["70:1","70:2","70:4","70:5"]},{"parent_channel":"[P1 70:1-18] Covering, Refuge, and Exposure to the Sky","parent_semantic_invariant":"Covering can shelter, conceal, or dignify, but judgment removes the interval between a body and the exposed heavens.","parent_surprising_reach":"Canopy, garment, fruit sheath, armor, tent, mountain pass, and high refuge all test distinct ways of being covered.","key":"B","name":"Refuge without overhead screen","reading_type":"mixed","scene_or_process":"A person seeks house, elevated ground, or walled shelter, yet remains exposed directly to the sky.","active_motifs":"uncovered exposure to heaven `quranic:root_000994:B004/m01`; converging into shelter `quranic:root_000070:B001/m01`; short defensive wall `quranic:root_001159:B007/m01`; clinging low to the ground `quranic:root_000025:B006/m01`; flood-safe elevation `quranic:root_001476:B004/m01`; elevated cloud or ground `quranic:root_001675:B016/m01`.","ayah_anchors":"`70:1,11` عَذَاب (`ع ذ ب`); `70:1` وَاقِع (`و ق ع`); `70:13` تُؤْوِيه، فَصِيلَتِه (`ء و ي`, `ف ص ل`); `70:14` الْأَرْض، يُنْجِيه (`ء ر ض`, `ن ج و`).","synthesis":"Refuge ranges from crouching at ground level to entering a wall and climbing above flood. ## Your response

Write in tr. Produce your response as three parts, in this order. If you return one combined response, label the parts clearly. If an orchestrator asks you to write the parts into separate files, omit wrapper labels from the prose and evidence files; the file path already supplies the label.

1. **The prose** — per the output contract in `COMMENTARY_SPEC.md` §5 and the task document above: continuous, single voice, no provenance markers, no headers named after evidence layers.
2. **The evidence surface** — separate from the prose, addressable per phrase, per `PRINCIPLES.md` §12.
3. A section headed exactly `=== PROMPT FRICTION ===` reporting honestly where the specification above was unclear, self-contradictory, underspecified, or impossible to follow, and any point where you had to invent a rule to proceed. Report this section every time, even if the run went cleanly — say so explicitly if you found nothing. This is how the specification gets improved.

## V2 Rendering Profile — 5.6 Sol High

This run tests whether `5.6-sol-high` can increase lexical depth while preserving
reader-facing clarity.

Increase depth through semantic precision, not additional bulk.

Before drafting, silently build a coverage ledger:

- include every `must_integrate` topic;
- include every `candidate` with a reader payoff not already expressed;
- if `v12_reader_responses` is absent because the default workflow retired
  per-ayah focus runs, do not infer a `stage_00` isolated response or
  confidence movement;
- if `v12_focus_trace_hermetic` is present, use its `baseline_models`,
  `context_deltas`, and `surprising_valid_outliers` as reconstructed
  before/after evidence. Do not call it a staged reveal transcript. Preserve
  surprising outliers when they remain anchored in this ayah, especially
  secondary split-root activations;
- include a regular reader-walk, plus/minus-5 reader-walk, whole-surah reading,
  or cross-run-publication item only when it adds a distinct retrospective
  insight or coverage check;
- use branch inventories to support explanations; they create no standalone
  prose obligation. When fallback inventories are present, treat them as
  restricted to this ayah's roots and anchored citations, not as a whole-surah
  obligation.
- treat `root_lexicon` as full dictionary/gloss support, not branch-filtered.
  It uses QAC-to-Furuq root mapping; when a QAC root is split, non-dominant
  Furuq targets are included as additional root entries and recorded in
  coverage. If multiple QAC roots map to the same Furuq root, the shared entry
  records all source roots in `qac_roots_ar` / `qac_root_mappings`; use those
  fields for evidence attribution when present, not the legacy single `root_ar`
  alone. Dictionary/gloss branches are evidence support, not independent
  obligations, unless tied to this ayah's word, topic, reader payoff, or cited
  relation. Full field means all distinct reader payoffs from activated
  material, not every dictionary branch for the root.
- if `channel_generated_outputs` lists quran-data files and this run gives you
  file access, read only those listed files when channel-family/path detail is
  necessary. Treat them as candidate/family/path evidence, not as an adjudicated
  channel ledger. State B channel restrictions still apply.

For each critical word, preserve these distinct layers when available:

1. local grammatical work;
2. locally selected sense;
3. coherent pressure supplied by activated or cited root branches;
4. one form, sound, rarity, or variant observation with a unique payoff;
5. later contextual change, compressed to its final reader-visible result.

Do not flatten these layers into a general metaphor. Deepen generic summaries by
naming the exact lexical mechanism. Ordering grammar and local meaning first is
grounding, not truth-ranking.

At first mention of an ayah word, use a structured Arabic surface span:
`{ar:surface_form, tr:Turkish-readable transliteration, gloss:target-language meaning}`.
Use the same full span again when the prose returns to that word after moving to
another word or another paragraph. Inside one short local sequence, after a full
span has just been given, a Turkish label or transliteration is enough. Explain
every technical term in the same sentence. Keep raw roots, root skeletons,
letter-by-letter root transliterations, and branch IDs in the evidence surface.
In prose, attach root discussion to the surface word:
`{ar:ٱلْعَادِيَاتِ, tr:el-âdiyât, gloss:koşup atılanlar} kelimesinin bağlı
olduğu kök alanı...`, not `ʿ-d-v kökü...`.

A paragraph may begin with the ayah's surface, a concrete image, or a
reader-facing claim. It may not begin with a bare grammar label or stacked
abstractions. Prefer "Âyet önce hamdi Allah'a verir; sabit ad cümlesi bu hamdi
yerleşik bir hüküm olarak taşır" over "Bu âyet, tek bir isim cümlesiyle yerleşik
bir hüküm kurar."

For this short ayah, keep at least two-thirds of the prose on its own wording.
Compress all later developments into at most three paragraphs and end with one
plain synthesis paragraph.

Use `v12_cross_run_publication`, if present, only as a compact coverage/priority
check derived from regular and plus/minus-5 reader runs. Do not copy it as prose,
and do not let it override local bundle evidence.

Use an explicit negative predicate only to correct a likely misconception,
protect the primary sense from replacement, or preserve live counter-evidence.
Default ceiling for this ayah: three. Never stack two negatives in one sentence.
During final revision, rewrite all other negatives as positive predication.
Do not drive negation to zero by default: if no explicit negative is needed,
include a profile-specific style audit in friction confirming that there was no
live misconception, replacement risk, or counter-evidence requiring one.

Pass only if every paragraph has one distinct reader payoff and the prose remains
clear with the evidence surface closed.
